Constitutional Chain\n\n```\nGoals → Intent → Product Definition → Requirements → Design → Code → Events → Projection → Delta\n                                                                                       ↓\n                                                                                  Scenarios\n                                                                                       ↓\n                                                                                Gap Analysis\n                                                                                       ↓\n                                                                    Repricing / New Goals / Intent\n```\n\n- **Goals** define the current overriding concerns for the active body of work.\n- **Intent** defines purpose and direction.\n- **Product Definition** defines the current product realization of the mutable source project, its key terms, and end-state shape in terms that can be decomposed into requirements.\n- **Requirements** define invariant truths the system must satisfy.\n- **Design** defines the structural decisions, interfaces, carrier surfaces, and delivery topology that make those truths achievable.\n- **ADRs** are one durable form of design record, not a second constitutional layer above design.\n- **Code** realizes those decisions.\n- **Events** record what actually happened.\n- **Projection** reconstructs current truth from the event stream.\n- **Delta** reveals drift between intended truth and realized state.\n- **Scenarios** test operational meaning — can the system actually do the thing the words describe?\n- **Gap analysis** identifies where real use cases hit the current model and reveal insufficiency.\n- **Repricing** updates goals, intent, product, requirements, design, or code when the system no longer harmonizes. When gap analysis reveals constitutional insufficiency, it generates new **Goals** and/or **Intent**.\n\nThis is the homeostatic loop. Every link in the chain is load-bearing. A break at any link — goals with no downstream repricing path, an unowned requirement, an ungrounded design record, code without a design decision — creates accidental law.\n\n`GOALS.md` is the bounded work-wave surface. It captures the current overriding concerns for the next body of work, keeps temporary focus out of deeper constitutional layers until it is intentionally repriced there, and is more immediate than intent while remaining higher-level than product definition and requirements.\n\n`PRODUCT.md` is the bridge between direction and obligation. It states the current product-definition surface of the mutable source project in present-tense terms, names the product terms and boundaries that downstream layers rely on, and provides the surface that requirements decompose.\n\n`PRODUCT.md` is not the immutable RC release artifact and not the installed product.\n\nThose belong to the recursive product taxonomy above.\n\nRequirements are the constitutional **what** of the project. They are not limited to user-visible product features. The live requirement surface may include capabilities, invariants, constraints, governance, and verification obligations. What matters is that each requirement states something the project must make true.\n\nDesign is the structural **how**. It chooses the concrete realization that satisfies requirement truth: interfaces, topology, file placement, carrier documents, entry/control surfaces, runtime wiring, and lawful tenant boundaries. Requirements may require such surfaces to exist and be delivered; design chooses where and how they are realized unless the path itself is constitutional.\n\nFor an STDO-defined product, the split remains exact regardless of physical\nlayout:\n\n- the definition's `what` bindings locate the shared constitutional `WHAT`\n- the definition's `how.build_tenants` bindings locate one or more independent\n  `HOW` realizations of that shared `WHAT`\n- tenant-local realization is derivative unless and until the governing truth is ratified in specification\n\n---\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":5630,"memberDigest":"sha256:80a66946d4767b1ff857aad4bbaba696b591cd7e7529324c2ece8ced9754ced5","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:9fac422af08221d1af95fa135e91178cb7c9d25137f2f77a3dd7a1a8900e1dcc","startByte":4130,"text":"## Probabilistic Work Boundary\n\nSpec-driven development exists to govern the boundary around work, not to absorb\nthe worker's internal solution strategy into the framework.\n\nFor any probabilistic, agentic, or delegated work unit, the live specification\nand design should declare:\n\n- the input and output contract\n- the required context\n- the capability or role expectation\n- the admissible evidence and evaluator regime\n- the provenance obligation\n- the lawful stop, hold, gap, continuation, or completion states\n\nThe worker, tool, agent, or domain implementation owns the internal HOW inside\nthat declared boundary unless the product explicitly promotes part of that HOW\ninto reusable declared structure.\n\nDeterministic checks and validation are evidence surfaces. They may optimize or\nprove a domain-local path where the domain can make part of the work precise.\nThey do not authorize the methodology, runtime, or framework to absorb domain\nsolution strategy as constitutional law.\n\nIn graph-native refinements such as `ODD_METHOD.md`, this boundary is usually\none vector or edge traversal. The traversal is the admissible external space.\nAny unconstrained part of a probabilistic worker's reasoning remains hidden\nworker-internal traversal and must be forced back through declared contracts,\nevidence, provenance, and control truth.\n\nIf a framework layer begins prescribing domain solution strategy beyond the\ndeclared work contract and control truth, it has crossed its authority boundary.\n\n---\n\n"},{"contextRef":"context://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/current-meaning@5","endByte":13840,"memberDigest":"sha256:895ea02f39bf1ec100e72084229f46ca49ec31cf456eaa0e0addc194cbe6e03b","memberRef":"member://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/specification/requirements/REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS.md","path":"specification/requirements/REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS.md","sourceLocator":"basis://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/9780971880110d0a1c107899aff2c3668150fcd87b4a13c47df7bc6e321d6adc","spanDigest":"sha256:895ea02f39bf1ec100e72084229f46ca49ec31cf456eaa0e0addc194cbe6e03b","startByte":0,"text":"# REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS - Generic Lifecycle Surface Model\n\n**Status**: Active\n**Category**: Product Requirement / Asset Model\n**Date**: 2026-06-28\n**Re-entry**: [T-043 D1-D3](../../.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-043-deliver-generic-live-llm-scenario-mvp.md#current-delivery-plan)\n**Derives from**: [GOALS.md](../GOALS.md), [INTENT.md](../INTENT.md),\n[PRODUCT.md](../PRODUCT.md),\n[T-001](../../.ai-workspace/tickets/completed/T-001-govern-minimal-odd-glc-requirements-and-graph-design.md),\n`/Users/jim/src/apps/abiogenesis/.ai-workspace/comments/codex/20260626T011328Z_STRATEGY_requirements_algebra_edge_spans.md`\n**Boundary reference**:\n`/Users/jim/src/apps/odd_sdlc/specification/PRODUCT.md`\n**Related families**:\n[REQ-GLC-BOUNDARY-AUTHORITY](REQ-GLC-BOUNDARY-AUTHORITY.md),\n[REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION](REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION.md)\n\n---\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine the generic lifecycle surface vocabulary required for `odd_glc` to\ninterpret governed work at any scale.\n\nThe Hello World binding below illustrates the minimum surface roles. A selected\nfull-input lifecycle also derives Intent/Product, Requirements and Design\nbefore construction, and preserves its obligations through evidence and\nrevision. The scenario is a proof vehicle, not the generic Product scope.\n\nThis family keeps `odd_glc` minimal. It imports the useful asset distinction\nfrom the current `odd_sdlc` product surface, but it does not import SDLC phase\nflow, software-domain policy, local ledgers, retry loops, release/deployment\nlaw, or product-local runtime truth.\n\n## Scope\n\nThis requirement covers generic lifecycle surfaces and their minimum closure\nmeaning. The same surface model shall apply to a task, project, program,\nmulti-product portfolio, or operating domain. Some surfaces are native\nodd_glc vocabulary or policy labels. Others are query labels over admitted\nGTL/ABG carriers. A `ViewAsset` name is not a license to mint a native carrier.\n\n- GTL owns graph, node, graph-vector, graph-function, module, job, interface,\n  and asset-surface declaration law.\n- ABG owns traversal, runtime facts, event replay, payload admission,\n  requirement projection, evidence admission, assurance fold, continuation,\n  residual, and re-entry truth.\n- `odd_glc` owns lifecycle meaning, vocabulary, read-model interpretation,\n  proof interpretation, and downstream specialization contracts.\n\nThis requirement does not define a build tenant, CLI, worker, second runtime,\nrequirement compiler, writable ledger, retry controller, full SDLC route,\nrelease snapshot, deployment lane, or operational-return loop.\n\n## Generic Lifecycle Surface Set\n\nThe generic `odd_glc` lifecycle model starts with these surfaces:\n\n| Surface | Classification | GTL/ABG binding | Illustrative Hello World binding |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `LifeCycleWorksiteAsset` | odd_glc lifecycle scope label. | ABG run/worksite refs and GTL module/job refs where present. | One worksite label for the first steel-thread trace. |\n| `LifecycleContextAsset` | Query/label over staged context. | ABG `AuthorityContextFragment` and `routeContextConstraint`. | Request to produce an inspectable greeting program with executable proof. |\n| `IntentAsset` | Product-authored lifecycle meaning. | GTL/ABG refs used by requirement declaration and staged context. | A program exists and emits the declared greeting when run. |\n| `ProductDefinitionAsset` | Product-authored lifecycle meaning. | GTL/ABG refs used by requirement declaration and proof policy. | Observable behavior and accepted evidence shape for the target program. |\n| `RequirementSetAsset` | GTL declaration binding, not odd_glc-native requirements. | GTL requirement declarations or wrappers consumed through ABG requirements algebra. | Requirements for declared greeting output, executable target, and evidence binding. |\n| `RequirementEnvironmentViewAsset` | Query/label over ABG carrier. | ABG `EdgeRequirementEnvironment` from `buildEdgeRequirementEnvironment`. | Active requirement environment for the construction edge. |\n| `DestinationTopologyAsset` | Query/label over ABG carrier. | ABG `DestinationTopology`. | Minimal source/artifact topology and one execution path. |\n| `InstructionSetAsset` | Query/label over ABG projections plus lifecycle policy. | ABG requirement obligation, materialization-target, and execution-schedule projections. | Instructions to materialize the target artifact under active requirements. |\n| `TargetArtifactAsset` | Downstream artifact binding. | GTL asset surface and ABG admitted artifact refs. | Greeting program source or runnable artifact as a domain specialization binding. |\n| `CapabilityAsset` | Capability declaration label. | GTL/ABG capability carrier plus ABG actor/operator invocation. | Runtime or command contract used by ABG to run the target artifact. |\n| `EvidenceBindingAsset` | Query/label over ABG carrier. | ABG `RequirementEvidenceBinding` from `bindRequirementEvidence`. | ABG-admitted command, exit status, stdout/stderr, artifact ref, and digest sufficient for fold interpretation. |\n| `AssuranceFoldViewAsset` | Query/label over ABG carrier. | ABG `RequirementFoldProjection` and assurance-case projection. | Pass, partial, failed, or blocked closure view for the steel-thread requirements. |\n| `ResidualPressureViewAsset` | Query/label over ABG carrier. | ABG `RequirementResidualProjection` and attenuation classification. | Remaining pressure if output, executable proof, or evidence binding is incomplete. |\n| `ReentryDecisionAsset` | Lifecycle disposition label over ABG truth plus F_H policy. | ABG continuation, correction, re-entry, release, or block facts. | Route to release/readiness, repair, reprice, or block for the trace. |\n\nThe generic minimum surface model does not make separate test-suite, backlog,\nrelease snapshot, deployment, runtime-return, incident, maintenance, or SDLC\nphase assets mandatory. Later requirements or downstream products may add those\nassets by specialization when the lifecycle scope requires them.\n\nThe Design stage binds `DestinationTopologyAsset` and admitted design-choice\nand evidence-policy references. The stage names identify lifecycle meaning,\nnot new native carriers or functions. The application `ProductDefinitionAsset`\nand imported specification describe the governed subject; neither replaces the\nbuilder Product Definition or registers a scenario as another odd_glc Product.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-001**: `odd_glc` shall define lifecycle assets as typed product-meaning surfaces over GTL/ABG carriers, not as an imperative phase flow or controller state machine.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-002**: Every lifecycle asset type shall preserve stable identity, semantic type, provenance, and current convergence context.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-003**: The first steel-thread scenario shall instantiate only the surfaces listed in the Generic Lifecycle Surface Set unless a later requirement explicitly reprices that steel thread.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-004**: `LifecycleContextAsset` shall be a lifecycle query or label over ABG staged context fragments. Those fragments shall not become closeable requirements unless admitted as requirement pressure.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-005**: `RequirementSetAsset` shall contain product-authored WHAT pressure by binding GTL requirement-declaration carriers or wrappers consumed through ABG requirements algebra. It shall not become a product-local requirement representation, ledger, translator, or compiler.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-006**: `RequirementEnvironmentViewAsset` shall be a query or label over ABG `EdgeRequirementEnvironment` and admitted active requirements, spans, staged context, prior fold projections, and residual pressure. It shall not write requirement truth or become a native odd_glc carrier.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-007**: `DestinationTopologyAsset` shall label or reference ABG `DestinationTopology` for the selected traversal without silently rewriting the active WHAT pressure or minting a parallel topology carrier.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-008**: `InstructionSetAsset` shall carry a bounded construction handoff for one GTL/ABG graph-function traversal. It shall not become a hidden operator checklist or product-local execution loop.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-009**: `TargetArtifactAsset` shall be generic lifecycle vocabulary. The fact that the first binding is a Hello World program shall remain domain specialization data, not generic odd_glc software policy.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-010**: A side-effecting proof edge shall require a `CapabilityAsset` before executable evidence may close the target requirement pressure, and the side effect shall be invoked and admitted by ABG rather than by `odd_glc`.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-011**: `EvidenceBindingAsset` shall be a query or label over ABG `RequirementEvidenceBinding` and ABG-admitted evidence refs to active requirement projections. Path shape, local files, command success, or stdout alone shall not close the requirement outside ABG admission and fold truth.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-012**: `AssuranceFoldViewAsset` shall be a query or label over ABG assurance fold projection for lifecycle meaning. It shall not introduce a second closure enum, writable fold ledger, or native fold carrier.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-013**: `ResidualPressureViewAsset` shall be a query or label over ABG residual truth and shall not route retries, continuations, or re-entry as product-local runtime authority.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-014**: `ReentryDecisionAsset` shall interpret ABG-owned continuation, correction, re-entry, release, or block facts as lifecycle disposition. It shall not create a second controller.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-015**: A steel-thread trace shall be closeable only when ABG-admitted evidence proves the declared target behavior for the admitted target artifact under the active requirement projection and capability contract.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-016**: Future downstream specialization may add domain-specific asset roles, evidence expectations, and policy bindings, but those additions shall not replace or duplicate the minimum `odd_glc` lifecycle asset truth. `odd_glc` shall not author software-domain requirement, design, source, test, build, release, deployment, or runtime-return semantics as generic lifecycle law.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-017**: `odd_glc` shall not mint a native carrier when the same role is already carried by GTL/ABG. A proposed native carrier requires requirement evidence that no GTL/ABG carrier exists and that the surface is not a generic ODD system asset.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-018**: A full-input lifecycle shall bind\nthe complete owner-selected source set, member identities and digests, source\nroles and governing authority. It shall preserve normative, supporting,\nspeculative and conflicting material as such. A file inventory is source\nintegrity evidence, not proof that all semantic obligations were derived.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-019**: The lifecycle declaration shall\ncarry admitted predecessor and source references through Intent/Product,\nRequirements, Design, construction and evidence. Each derivation shall retain\napplicable obligations and expose new, unresolved or conflicting pressure.\nSelection of a bounded target shall not erase unimplemented obligations or\nsilently substitute a weaker fixture, summary or module contract.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-020**: Realization and proof shall bind\nthe same admitted source obligation and its current requirement/design basis.\nRealization evidence, verifier artifacts, verifier execution and semantic\nassessment shall retain distinct roles. A passing verifier for a weaker\ncontract shall not close the stronger source obligation.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-021**: Lifecycle interpretation shall\ndistinguish computations over admitted identities, roles, freshness and declared\ncoverage (C), admitted agent judgments of faithfulness and sufficiency (J), and\nowner scope and acceptance rulings (O). These are evaluation roles, not a new\ncarrier catalog. No C check shall infer semantic completeness from counts; no\nJ result shall mint admission or scope authority; no proposed scope change\nshall take effect without its permitted owner ruling.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-022**: A failed verifier or changed\nrequirement shall preserve the prior result and route affected work to its\nowning lifecycle stage through admitted ABG re-entry. Interpretation shall\ndistinguish stale affected evidence from reusable unaffected evidence, retain\npending obligations, and support reconstruction of that distinction from\npersisted truth. Resetting an instance shall not stand in for this revision.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-023**: A bounded lifecycle result shall\nname its exact completed claims and remaining source obligations. Full\napplication completion shall require all mandatory selected-source outcomes\nand constraints to be satisfied, or explicitly repriced by their permitted\nowner. Test counts, file counts, module names and prior bounded acceptance\nshall not close remaining application or release claims.\n\n**REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS-024**: Full-input lifecycle order,\ndependencies, evidence gates and re-entry shall be declared as one reusable\nGTL composition consumed by ABG. A host caller that invokes construction and\nexecution alone shall not establish the upstream lifecycle stages; adding\nhost-side stage invocation or a local requirement/proof ledger is prohibited.\n"},{"contextRef":"context://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/current-meaning@5","endByte":14008,"memberDigest":"sha256:23ab6a435eb3ff4c610f73b4a9569b43c24a86a7f160628ee3848a0fe23dc27e","memberRef":"member://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/specification/requirements/REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION.md","path":"specification/requirements/REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION.md","sourceLocator":"basis://odd-glc/fresh-native-config/9780971880110d0a1c107899aff2c3668150fcd87b4a13c47df7bc6e321d6adc","spanDigest":"sha256:23ab6a435eb3ff4c610f73b4a9569b43c24a86a7f160628ee3848a0fe23dc27e","startByte":0,"text":"# REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION - Upstream Readiness Gate\n\n**Status**: Active\n**Category**: Product Requirement / Consumption Contract\n**Date**: 2026-06-28\n**Re-entry**: [T-043 D1-D3](../../.ai-workspace/tickets/active/T-043-deliver-generic-live-llm-scenario-mvp.md#current-delivery-plan)\n**Derives from**: [GOALS.md](../GOALS.md), [INTENT.md](../INTENT.md),\n[PRODUCT.md](../PRODUCT.md),\n[T-001](../../.ai-workspace/tickets/completed/T-001-govern-minimal-odd-glc-requirements-and-graph-design.md),\n[REQ-GLC-BOUNDARY-AUTHORITY](REQ-GLC-BOUNDARY-AUTHORITY.md),\n`/Users/jim/src/apps/abiogenesis/specification/requirements/abg/REQ-R-ABG3-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA.md`,\n`/Users/jim/src/apps/abiogenesis/.ai-workspace/comments/codex/20260626T011328Z_STRATEGY_requirements_algebra_edge_spans.md`\n**Related families**:\n[REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS](REQ-GLC-LIFECYCLE-VOCABULARY-ASSETS.md)\n\n---\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine the ABG/GTL consumption contract and upstream readiness gate for\n`odd_glc` lifecycle bindings and queries that interpret admitted\nrequirements-algebra truth.\n\nThis family turns the bootstrap rule into requirement law: when ABG/GTL cannot\nanswer a lifecycle question from real admitted carriers and wired runtime\npaths, `odd_glc` shall mark the dependency missing or defer the affected\nfunction. It shall not compensate with a product-local peer ledger, compiler,\nfold, residual, retry, or re-entry controller.\n\n## Upstream Readiness States\n\n`odd_glc` classifies each consumed upstream capability before ratifying a\ndependent lifecycle binding or GTL composition declaration.\n\n| State | Meaning | Closure effect |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `ready` | Capability is present, release-pinned or otherwise explicitly admitted, callable from the expected runtime path, and proven by non-forgeable evidence. | Dependent binding may ratify if its local requirements are satisfied. |\n| `missing` | Capability is not present in the consumed GTL/ABG substrate. | Dependent binding shall defer or block. |\n| `placeholder` | Capability exists as a stub, partial carrier, compatibility alias, or prose claim without real runtime behavior. | Dependent binding shall not ratify or close. |\n| `test_only` | Capability is exercised only by tests or test-only proof inputs and has no non-test runtime caller or public path. | Dependent binding shall not ratify or close. |\n| `unwired` | Capability exists but is not connected to the required runtime, replay, admission, fold, residual, continuation, or query path. | Dependent binding shall not ratify or close. |\n| `unpinned` | Capability source or release snapshot is not identified enough for repeatable consumption. | Dependent binding shall not ratify or close. |\n\n## Genericity Rule\n\nA constructive function is a GTL/ABG system function when the same function is\nneeded by multiple ODD domains, such as software delivery, world modeling, and\ntrading evaluation. Generic ODD construction belongs upstream. `odd_glc` shall\nnot republish a generic GTL/ABG system function under a `glc.*` name.\n\n`odd_glc` may define lifecycle vocabulary, labels, policy overlays, F_P/F_H\nsurfaces, read/query interpretation, specialization contracts, and GTL\ncomposition declarations over GTL/ABG system functions. It shall not define a\nnative graph-function catalog for generic lifecycle construction.\n\n## Bound System Functions And Carriers\n\nThe first review target binds lifecycle slots to GTL/ABG system functions and\ncarriers. These bindings are consumption declarations, not native odd_glc\ngraph functions.\n\nThe symbol names below are historical capability locators, not an ABIogenesis\n5 callable selection or availability assertion. Each activation shall resolve\nits exact current owner contract and readiness in the selected HOW. The owning\n[ABG requirement-proof carry-through law](../../../abiogenesis/specification/requirements/abg/REQ-R-ABG3-REQUIREMENT-PROOF-CARRY-THROUGH.md)\ngoverns shared obligation identity, evidence roles, replay coverage and\nnon-closing residuals; its declaration alone does not prove an installed path.\n\n| Lifecycle slot | GTL/ABG system function or carrier | Existing or expected ABI surface | Non-closure examples |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| context observation and gap routing | `abg.requirements.ingest_context_fragments` / `abg.requirements.route_context_constraint` | `routeContextConstraint` | odd_glc admits or routes staged context locally. |\n| requirement authoring | GTL requirement-declaration carrier or wrapper preserving requirement identity, relations, spans, context refs, evidence-policy refs, and projection refs for ABG admission | GTL declaration and ABG admission surfaces | `RequirementSetAsset` holds an odd_glc-native structure that must later be translated into ABG terms. |\n| requirement environment projection | `abg.requirements.compile_edge_environment` | `buildEdgeRequirementEnvironment` | Span matching is substring-based, narrowing-only, test-only, or not wired to runtime query. |\n| requirement graph/refinement | `abg.requirements.derive_requirement_graph` / `abg.requirements.refine_goal` | T-162 requirement carriers | odd_glc decomposes WHAT through a native constructive function. |\n| edge obligations and work pressure | `abg.requirements.project_edge_obligations` | `projectRequirements`, `projectMaterializationTargets`, `projectExecutionSchedules` | odd_glc constructs local obligation, materialization, or schedule ledgers. |\n| destination topology | ABG `DestinationTopology` carrier | ABG destination-topology carrier | Destination topology silently rewrites active requirement meaning or becomes an odd_glc carrier. |\n| side-effecting capability and execution | GTL/ABG capability carrier plus ABG-owned actor/operator invocation | ABG actor/operator and payload admission surfaces | odd_glc shells out, supervises, retries, or treats a local command contract as runtime truth. |\n| evidence binding | `abg.requirements.bind_evidence` | `bindRequirementEvidence` | Evidence is inferred from local files, stdout text, path shape, or test harness assertions without ABG admission. |\n| assurance fold and assurance case | `abg.requirements.fold_requirement_state` / `abg.requirements.project_assurance_case` | `foldRequirementEvidence`, `projectAssuranceCase` | Fold state is forgeable, substring-derived, test-only, or not callable from a runtime path. |\n| residual and attenuation | residual output of `abg.requirements.fold_requirement_state` plus attenuation classification | `residualizeRequirementFolds`, `classifyRequirementAttenuation` | Residuals are represented by local compatibility wrappers or narrowing-only placeholders. |\n| re-entry disposition | ABG continuation, correction, re-entry, release, or block facts tied to runtime/replay truth | ABG continuation and re-entry truth | odd_glc decides retry, continuation, or re-entry through a local controller or checklist. |\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n**REQ-GLC-ABG-REQUIREMENTS-ALGEBRA-CONSUMPTION-001**: `odd_glc` shall classify every consumed GTL/ABG requirements-algebra capability as Given **Goals**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Intent** surface.\n2. Given **Intent**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Product** definition.\n3. Given **Product**, a competent team should be able to derive conformant **Requirements**.\n4. Given **Requirements**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Design**.\n5. Given **Requirements + Design**, a competent team should be able to derive conformant **Code**.\n\nThis does not mean the derivation is unique. Different designs may satisfy the same requirements, and different codebases may satisfy the same design. The test is sufficiency, not determinism.\n\nFailures at any boundary indicate a specification defect:\n\n- If intent cannot be derived from goals, the goals surface is unclear, contradictory, or not concrete enough to orient the next bounded wave of work.\n- If product cannot be derived from intent, the intent surface is too vague, contradictory, or not concrete enough to guide realization.\n- If requirements cannot be derived from product, the product surface is underspecified, contradictory, or not decomposable enough to govern implementation.\n- If design cannot be derived from requirements, the requirement surface is underspecified, contradictory, or polluted with accidental implementation detail.\n- If code cannot be derived from requirements plus design, the design surface is incomplete, ambiguous, or not operational enough.\n\nThe purpose of ADRs and design documents is therefore not decorative explanation. They are the load-bearing bridge between constitutional truth and executable realization.\n\nReconstruction sufficiency also depends on ambiguity governance. If a boundary\ncan be crossed only by hiding a major unresolved ambiguity, the upstream surface\nis not yet sufficient even if some downstream artifact can be produced.\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":112016,"memberDigest":"sha256:80a66946d4767b1ff857aad4bbaba696b591cd7e7529324c2ece8ced9754ced5","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:bfc29fb5fb2945408e52d77def912541eb35ddcac3bccf1c1faa4282b714a621","startByte":110225,"text":"## Requirement Categories\n\nEvery live requirement family shall be categorized by what kind of truth it asserts. This prevents the common mistake of treating the entire requirement surface as if it were only a feature list.\n\n| Category | Meaning | Typical question |\n|----------|---------|------------------|\n| **Capability** | A behavior, function, or surface the system must provide | \"What can the system do?\" |\n| **Constraint / Guarantee** | An invariant, law, safety property, portability rule, or semantic guarantee that bounds how the system may behave | \"What must always remain true?\" |\n| **Governance** | A policy or control rule over lifecycle, authority, visibility, release, or operator action | \"What governs use and change?\" |\n| **Verification** | A requirement on tests, evidence, traceability, qualification, or forensic surfaces | \"How do we know the claim is true?\" |\n\nRequirements remain part of the constitutional layer in all four cases. A category is not a priority label and not a claim that the requirement is user-facing. It is a statement of what kind of project truth the requirement owns.\n\nThe important distinction is:\n\n- Requirements describe the full constitutional **what**.\n- Design describes the structural **how**. ADRs are one durable design form.\n- Scenarios validate the operational meaning of capability claims.\n\nTherefore, not every requirement should be translated into a marketed \"feature.\" Some requirements are better expressed as guarantees, constraints, governance rules, or verification obligations.\n\nRequirements should also avoid freezing concrete realization choices prematurely. Unless a path, filename, or packaging rule is itself part of the constitutional truth, those choices belong to design rather than the requirement surface.\n\n---\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":116378,"memberDigest":"sha256:80a66946d4767b1ff857aad4bbaba696b591cd7e7529324c2ece8ced9754ced5","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:8e692ada13790c09080df6d1b46c27262b38ed6583a53f66e4cd43282231eccc","startByte":114008,"text":"## Design Rule\n\nRequirements state what control or delivery surfaces must exist.\n\nDesign decides the concrete mechanism that realizes them.\n\nIf a technology stack, carrier, or runtime is not already constrained by an\nupstream constitutional surface, choosing it is part of design rather than a\nseparate upstream authority.\n\nThat boundary matters especially for agent-facing bootstrap and control surfaces:\n\n- requirements may say that authoritative bootstrap or control surfaces must be present, carry bare axioms, and route to the canonical method\n- design decides whether those surfaces are entry documents, embedded blocks, standalone compiled artifacts, or another lawful topology\n\nIf filenames and placement are hardcoded in requirements without constitutional necessity, the requirement surface has absorbed design detail and made re-derivation harder.\n\n---\n\n## Renewal Path\n\nIntent is not only top-down. The full homeostatic cycle includes a reverse path where real use cases generate new intent:\n\n```\nCurrent spec → real-world use case → gap analysis → new goals / intent\n```\n\nThis is how the system stays alive instead of becoming a frozen constitution. The generative rule:\n\n1. A real use case (scenario, deployment, external review) hits the current model\n2. Gap analysis identifies what the constitution cannot express\n3. If the gap is constitutional (not just a missing implementation), new goals and/or intent are written\n4. The repriced surface then flows forward through the chain: product → requirements → design → code\n\nNew intents emerge from repeated, real use-case pressure against the current model — not from abstract speculation. The gap must be concrete before it becomes intent. Ad hoc coding pressure does not generate intent; explicit gap analysis does.\n\nThis flow is not a one-pass waterfall computation. It is a cumulative iteration:\n\n- requirements and design are refined over time\n- code is repeatedly re-derived against the current constitutional surface\n- gaps discovered during implementation, testing, replay, or scenario work feed back into repricing\n\nWhat must remain stable is the full direction of authority: goals govern intent, intent governs product, product governs requirements, requirements govern design, and requirements plus design govern code, even when the project advances through many iterations.\n\n---\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":23161,"memberDigest":"sha256:6fb49e186c15a3ebd48dec6b2728a397f1cd5199c4c0d112a0d0c70a2d6346fc","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","path":"standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:aaded0399d06c6acb8ab896822bc6da237e0e84c9bb78f5b3e09f3ecd269e4a4","startByte":11353,"text":"## 4B. Ontology-First Design Rule\n\nEvery new or materially changed active semantic boundary must derive its\nsemantic model from one accepted **Ontology**. Before a new or changed entity,\nfunction, authority, effect, Prime-conformant carrier, module boundary, public\noperation, or schema is promoted, the affected boundary must first produce a\ncandidate Ontology slice and carry it through the acceptance sequence below.\n\nThe Ontology is the prior **semantic-design authority** for the boundary. It is\nsubordinate to constitutional `WHAT`: it derives from intent, product,\nrequirements, and applicable graph or domain method. It does not invent product\nmeaning. Once accepted, it governs semantic entities, relationships,\ninvariants, lifecycle, authority, functions, composition, and effects.\n\nThe Ontology does not replace target architecture or module design. Accepted\ndesign remains the structural `HOW` for topology, interfaces, carrier placement,\nmodule boundaries, algorithms, and local realization. Implementation and tests\nderive from requirements plus accepted design and must preserve the accepted\nOntology; they are not direct substitutes for either layer.\n\nOntology acceptance must identify the constitutional sources it derives from\nand the design authority that accepted it. It must carry stable element or\nrelation identities plus a version or basis that makes source change,\nstaleness, and the affected projection set decidable. A reprice of a governing\nsource invalidates only the affected Ontology relations and downstream\nprojections until their impact is re-evaluated. Code, tests, generated schemas,\nor a single implementing agent cannot self-ratify missing Ontology authority.\nThe Ontology verdict may live in the same design pack and acceptance record as\nthe three-view verdict; this rule does not require a second document, ticket, or\napproval ceremony.\n\nProportionality applies. A **material semantic boundary** is one whose change\naffects identity, authority, lifecycle, public contract, externally observable\nProduct meaning, cross-module topology, or accepted effect or closure law. A\n`realization_refactor` wholly inside an accepted boundary may cite the existing\nOntology and design basis and prove `no ontology delta`; it does not require\nre-authoring or re-accepting unchanged evidence. A semantic or structural\nchange must publish only the affected Ontology/design delta and its projection\nimpact set. Unaffected maintenance and unrelated boundaries do not freeze\nmerely because one source digest changed.\n\n### Proportional Design Sequencing\n\nFor one governed boundary `B`, let `M(B)` be its complete material-relation set\ndefined under Decision-Complete Symbolic Design, and let `U(B)` be the\nunresolved members of that set:\n\n```text\nU(B) = unresolved(M(B))\ndecision_complete(B) iff\n  U(B) = empty\n  and M(B) is jointly satisfiable\n  and every network admitted by M(B) is semantically equivalent under the\n      governing Product, requirements, and accepted design relations\nco_evolution_admissible(B) iff decision_complete(B)\ndesign_gate(B) =\n  the smallest causally closed affected set needed to establish\n  decision_complete(B)\n```\n\nDesign sequencing follows `decision_complete(B)` rather than one global\nauthoring order:\n\n- **co-evolution** is lawful only when `decision_complete(B)` holds. Design\n  still owns the structural `HOW`, but design, implementation, and tests may\n  evolve together and reconcile before promotion or closure;\n- **design-gated** sequencing is required when implementation would otherwise\n  establish an unresolved or contradictory relation, or choose between\n  materially non-equivalent networks, with durable consequences for future\n  change. The affected design decision must be accepted before retained\n  implementation establishes it. A disposable spike may supply evidence\n  without becoming the selected design.\n\nNeither mode is the global default. The same proportionality relation selects\nbetween them. Proportionality decides which relations are material and bounds\nthe smallest affected gate; decision completeness is the resulting\nadmissibility test. Where a bounded symbolic model can contract the material\nsemantic alternatives within effective reasoning capacity, the code,\nruntime-state, test, review, and reconciliation space thereby removed is\ncounterfactual evidence of that contraction, not an independent reason for a\ndesign gate. Where no material alternative remains, a separate prior gate adds\nceremony without disambiguation.\n\nThe bidirectional design-and-implementation relation is an evidence relation,\nnot bidirectional semantic authority. Implementation may provide a\ncounterexample, falsify a constraint, or expose hidden ambiguity. It may not\nselect the missing semantic answer, rewrite upstream truth to legalize itself,\nor ratify a material design decision after establishing it.\n\nThe selected relation and its basis belong in the existing work or design\ncarrier; this rule does not require a separate artifact. If implementation\nexposes previously hidden architectural ambiguity, the boundary switches to\ndesign-gated sequencing at the smallest causally closed affected decision set\nrather than retroactively invalidating unrelated work. Serial implementation\nrepairs do not establish design acceptance. Repeated or cross-cutting material\nsurprises are evidence that the affected design cut is underconstrained and\nrequire one bounded `design_reframe`; retained code remains provisional\nevidence until that reframe is accepted.\n\nThis applies the governing proportionality law: a prior design gate is\nwarranted when it removes material semantic ambiguity whose alternatives would\nestablish materially different durable architecture, and that contraction\njustifies its reasoning load. It is disproportionate when upstream truth leaves\nno materially divergent architecture to adjudicate and a separate prior\nacceptance would add no disambiguation. Design still records and owns the\nresulting `HOW` before promotion or closure.\n\nFor this method:\n\n```text\nOntology =\n  entities\n  + identities\n  + relationships\n  + invariants\n  + lifecycle\n  + authority\n  + atomic functions\n  + higher-order composition\n  + effects\n  + projections\n```\n\nA class inventory, schema list, endpoint list, command list, or carrier census\nalone is not an Ontology.\n\n### Decision-Complete Symbolic Design\n\nFor one governed boundary `B`, the complete material-relation set is:\n\n```text\nM(B) =\n  entities\n  + identities\n  + relationships\n  + cardinalities\n  + invariants\n  + authorities\n  + authority transitions\n  + functions\n  + higher-order composition laws\n  + topology\n  + lifecycle states and transitions\n  + public contracts\n  + externally observable Product meaning\n  + declared domains and admission conditions\n  + failure and refusal routes\n  + effect and closure laws\n  + projections\n  + module mappings\n  + material algorithmic obligations\n```\n\nA design boundary is **decision-complete** when `U(B)` is empty, `M(B)` forms\none jointly satisfiable constraint network, and every network admitted by\n`M(B)` is semantically equivalent under the governing Product, requirements,\nand accepted design relations.\n\nOntology, Prime contraction, IACS, target design, domain/sequence/state views,\npublic contracts, module boundaries, implementation, and tests are projections\nof that one network at different reasoning altitudes. Their presence, section\nnames, individual plausibility, or mechanical validity does not establish\ndecision completeness.\n\nThe boundary is underconstrained whenever `decision_complete(B)` is false,\nincluding when implementation must introduce a new material semantic decision,\nprojections contradict one another, or two materially non-equivalent networks\nremain admissible. Local algorithm and layout choices may remain open only when\nevery admitted choice is semantically equivalent under the accepted network.\nContradictory projections make the design unsatisfiable and block promotion.\n\nDecision completeness does not require another document. The constraint\nnetwork may be expressed through the existing Ontology, algebra, module mapping,\nand views when those surfaces jointly carry the complete relation.\n\n### Bounded Frame Conservation (`STDO-UP-017`)\n\n`M(B)` closes one governed boundary. A bounded local action is taken inside a\nframe that derives from other accepted frames, and its lawfulness depends on\nrelations the acting frame does not itself own.\n\n`Global` and `local` are relative to the selected frame. Each material design\nframe must identify:\n\n- the governing frames and accepted relations from which it derives;\n- the bounded affected relation set of the proposed action;\n- the applicable inherited invariants;\n- the authority and composition seams crossing that set; and\n- the subordinate frames opened by unresolved material distinctions.\n\nA frame may derive from more than one governing frame. A persistence component\nbelongs to an entity lifecycle boundary and an operational consistency\nboundary; an admission function belongs to a domain transition and a\nruntime-authority boundary; a shared callable may be consumed by several\nsibling domains without becoming subordinate to one. A single governing frame\nis lawful where the structure is genuinely hierarchical, and is not required.\nGoverning frames form a constraint network for the same reason `M(B)` does.\n\nEvery seam crossing the affected set must either include its causally relevant\nfar-side relation in that set, or state why that relation cannot affect, and\ncannot be affected by, the bounded action. This makes the declared set testable\nagainst its own declared seams without requiring a closure algorithm or\nrecursive expansion through irrelevant relations.\n\nThe result is evaluated against every affected governing frame, preserves each\napplicable accepted relation, and updates only affected subordinate\nprojections. A bounded local action conserves governing truth; it does not\nrewrite it. A result that changes a governing relation re-enters at that\nrelation before acceptance.\n\nA subordinate frame opens only when a material distinction cannot be resolved\nat the governing altitude. This rule does not require recursively documenting\nirrelevant descendants.\n\nThis law is false if a change is locally valid inside the selected frame but\nviolates, duplicates, or silently reinterprets an invariant in any affected\ngoverning frame.\n\nDiscovered functionality is evidence about required behavior. It is not yet an\narchitectural operation. A discovered verb, screen action, endpoint, command,\nticket phrase, or use case shall not be promoted directly into a public\noperation identity, peer carrier, module, or implementation function. It must\nfirst be derived through the Ontology.\n\nThe required derivation order is:\n\n```text\nconstitutional functionality\n  -> candidate Ontology\n  -> logical completeness\n  -> whole-family Prime contraction\n  -> accepted Ontology basis\n  -> Irreducible Architectural Carrier Set\n  -> target design and domain, sequence, and state projections\n  -> public contract and adapter projections\n  -> implementation and test projections\n```\n\n### Native Constructability Before Design Acceptance (`STDO-UP-002`)\n\nA semantically coherent design is not acceptable when its selected substrate\ncannot natively construct the required identities, relationships, transitions,\nauthority boundaries, effects, lifecycle, composition, publication, and\nretirement behavior.\n\nA missing substrate capability remains an explicit design gap or triggers\nlawful re-entry. It must not be hidden behind a reference bridge, adapter,\nfilesystem runner, test double, or promised future feature. This is a\nconstructability obligation, not a prescribed implementation; the consumer\nchooses the substrate and evidence mechanism.\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":71900,"memberDigest":"sha256:6fb49e186c15a3ebd48dec6b2728a397f1cd5199c4c0d112a0d0c70a2d6346fc","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","path":"standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:7ca71952fb9784c33be92f5e3353dc1c33252ca2ee1d11ca58628055c142c58f","startByte":55679,"text":"## 5E. Ontology And Three-View Behavioral Design Gate\n\nEvery new or materially changed semantic or typed module boundary must carry\none accepted Ontology and one complete three-view Mermaid design asset before\nthe boundary is promoted or claims design-method closure. When\n`co_evolution_admissible(B)` holds, design evidence, implementation, and tests\nmay develop together before that gate. Otherwise the affected unresolved,\ncontradictory, or materially non-equivalent relation is accepted before\nretained implementation establishes it. Unchanged boundaries cite their\naccepted basis; local realization work with no material semantic-boundary delta\ndoes not recreate this gate.\n\nThe asset must contain all three views:\n\n1. a Mermaid `classDiagram` domain model;\n2. a Mermaid `sequenceDiagram` execution model; and\n3. a Mermaid `stateDiagram-v2` lifecycle model.\n\nA project may ratify another text-native diagram format only when it preserves\nthe same three distinct views and the same cross-view checks. One broad flowchart\ndoes not substitute for the three models.\n\nThe three views may be sections of one existing design surface and may share\none acceptance decision. This rule does not require three files, three tickets,\nthree reviews, or three approval ceremonies.\n\nThe views are not decorative and they are not peer truth surfaces. They are\nfidelity-checked projections of the accepted Ontology. Together they are the\nsign-off surface for domain identity, authority, behavior, lifecycle, and axiom\nconformance.\n\n### Ontology evidence\n\nThe design asset must cite one accepted Ontology for the active boundary and\ninclude or reference its:\n\n- entity and relationship inventory;\n- invariant and cardinality law;\n- entity-lifecycle completeness matrix;\n- authority matrix;\n- atomic-function and higher-order-function derivation matrix;\n- governing composition and effect algebra where applicable;\n- whole-family Prime contraction, including every accepted atom's admitted\n  domain and realization-projection mapping; and\n- explicit deferred, excluded, and unresolved functionality.\n\nThe Ontology may be a boundary-bounded slice of a wider accepted product\nOntology. The slice must cite its parent and preserve parent identity,\nrelationship, lifecycle, authority, and function law. Copying selected rows into\na new local model without that derivation creates another truth surface.\n\n### Invariant Reconstruction Sufficiency (`STDO-UP-018`)\n\nThe evidence above is not a documentation deliverable. It is the durable\nreasoning state a bounded actor needs, because the actor selecting a local\naction generally does not hold the reasoning that produced the governing\nframes. Dialogue, commentary, prior-agent memory, and implementation folklore\nare not that state.\n\nGiven an accepted material-frame basis, as determined by the existing material\nsemantic boundary definition, and a proposed bounded action, the selected STDO\nbasis together with the accepted design must be sufficient to recover every\ngoverning relation in the action's causally closed affected set, and to decide\nwhether the action preserves, changes, duplicates, or violates each of those\nrelations.\n\nThe two inputs are distinct and neither substitutes for the other. The selected\nmethod describes which relations are required and how to reason about them; the\naccepted design supplies the concrete invariant values. A design that restates\nfederal decision law to satisfy this rule reproduces the local-method\nduplication this law exists to remove.\n\nThe four decisions are distinguished as follows:\n\n| Decision | The action, against a governing relation |\n|---|---|\n| preserves | leaves the relation intact and operates inside it |\n| changes | alters the relation itself, and therefore re-enters at that relation before acceptance |\n| duplicates | establishes an equivalent path that is explicitly disposed as a constituent, replica, replacement, or deliberately independent realization |\n| violates | contradicts the relation, or establishes an equivalent path that is left undisposed |\n\nThe boundary between `changes` and `violates` is the locus of acceptance, not\nthe size of the delta: an otherwise lawful change accepted inside the acting\nframe rather than at the relation it changes is a violation. The boundary\nbetween `duplicates` and `violates` is disposition, not equivalence: an\nequivalent path is lawful when disposed and a defect when not.\n\nSufficiency is bound to a declared action and its affected set. It is not a\nclaim over an unbounded space of possible future changes, and the performance\nof any particular reviewer is not evidence either way.\n\nThis obligation adds no artifact. It is satisfied through the Ontology\nevidence, authority matrix, transformation-admission relation, frame\ndeclaration, views, and module mapping already required, and is a property of\nthat material rather than a separate package.\n\nThis law is false if, for a declared bounded action, the accepted design does\nnot expose a governing relation in that action's causally closed affected set,\nor the preserve, change, duplicate, or violate decision cannot be reached from\nthe selected STDO basis and accepted design, without conversation history,\ncommentary, prior-worker memory, or implementation folklore.\n\nWhere the sufficiency of a boundary is tested, the test uses named cases with\nknown governing relations and expected decisions, evaluated with conversation\nhistory, commentary, prior-worker memory, and implementation folklore withheld.\n\n### Domain model\n\nThe `classDiagram` is the structural domain projection of the Ontology. It must\nshow, for the active boundary:\n\n- domain identities and cardinalities rather than helper-class decomposition;\n- entity ownership, invariants, and lawful lifecycle relationships;\n- atomic function families and their entity inputs and outputs where they cross\n  the active boundary;\n- higher-order composition relationships where they determine boundary shape;\n- IACS carriers and their Prime-atom projection mappings;\n- subordinate payloads;\n- effect-edge-only payloads;\n- downstream-only projections;\n- deferred families outside the active slice;\n- authoritative versus downstream role;\n- public versus module-local visibility; and\n- composition, containment, association, and real variant-family structure.\n\nThe standard stereotype vocabulary is:\n\n- `<<prime-conformant>>`\n- `<<subordinate>>`\n- `<<effect-edge>>`\n- `<<deferred>>`\n- `<<authoritative>>`\n- `<<downstream>>`\n\nThe standard visibility vocabulary is:\n\n- `+` public or exported\n- `-` module-local or private\n\nThe standard relationship expectations are:\n\n- composition for owned subordinate payloads;\n- association for downstream consumption; and\n- inheritance only for real variant or outcome families.\n\n### Sequence model\n\nThe `sequenceDiagram` is the behavioral projection of Ontology functions and\nauthority. It must show the supported execution path from admitted input to\nresult or truthful stop. It must name the owner and required authority of every\ndecision, admission, execution, projection, and effect boundary. Where\napplicable it must show malformed input or output, retry, recursion,\nfan-out/fan-in, nested workflow, and human escalation paths.\n\nEvery participant must exist in the domain model or be an explicitly external\nactor. Every message must bind to a declared carrier transform, graph/C\nconstructor, interpreter action, or effect-handler call. A private loop,\nservice method, plugin, shell, or script may not silently replace a declared\nworkflow transition.\n\nEvery message must also bind to an Ontology function and its admitted authority\nrole. Actor identity or call reachability alone does not authorize a message.\n\n### State model\n\nThe `stateDiagram-v2` is the lifecycle projection of Ontology entities. It must\nshow the complete admitted lifecycle for the active boundary, including\nrefusal, blocked, continuation, retry, escalation, and terminal states that the\nboundary can produce. Every transition must bind to an Ontology function and\nname its owning authority, admission, compiler, interpreter, event, projection,\nor external act.\n\nController-local memory is not a lawful source of lifecycle truth when the\nproduct declares replay, event, graph, or carrier ownership.\n\n### Cross-view axiom evaluation\n\nThe design pack must evaluate every applicable product, graph, language,\nruntime, handler, and module axiom with one of:\n\n- `pass`;\n- `fail`; or\n- `not_applicable`, with a reason.\n\nThe minimum evaluation columns are:\n\n| Axiom | Ontology evidence | Authority | Domain evidence | Sequence evidence | State evidence | Native enforcement | Admission/compiler enforcement | Verdict | Gap owner |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n\nThe cross-view evaluation must prove that:\n\n- every element in each view derives from the accepted Ontology;\n- every discovered functionality row has a derived, deferred, excluded, or gap\n  disposition;\n- every sequence participant and lifecycle carrier exists in the domain model;\n- every sequence message has a declared semantic or effect boundary;\n- every lifecycle transition is derivable from admitted truth;\n- every function and transition has an explicit authority path and composition\n  does not widen that authority implicitly;\n- every public operation is a projection of an Ontology function rather than an\n  independently authored semantic peer;\n- raw probabilistic output cannot transition directly to accepted or closed;\n- handlers and plugins own interiors only when the runtime owns admission,\n  events, continuation, and closure;\n- native types enforce locally decidable relations; and\n- admission or semantic compilation owns global references, completeness, and\n  realization gaps.\n\nFor graph-native work, every batch, retry, recursion, or nested workflow must\nbe visible through the product's declared graph or compute algebra. A relied-on\n`semantic_not_realized` constructor is a blocking gap. It may not be replaced\nby imperative glue in a plugin, shell, service, script, or test harness.\n\n### Gate and retrospective work\n\nFor a new or semantically changed boundary, the Ontology and design verdict must\nboth be `accepted` before the affected implementation, public contract, or\nsemantic carrier is promoted or the work claims design-method closure. Any\nfailed applicable axiom, missing functionality disposition, unresolved\nauthority path, or blocking realization gap keeps the verdict non-accepted.\n\nFor an unchanged `realization_refactor`, the gate is proportional: cite the\naccepted Ontology and design basis, identify the touched projection set, and\nprove that entities, relationships, lifecycle, authority, functions, effects,\nand public semantics have no delta. That proof reuses the existing verdict; it\ndoes not create a new Ontology or acceptance ceremony.\n\nWhen `co_evolution_admissible(B)` holds, implementation may precede or develop\nwith design evidence and may be retained after reconciliation. Until the\ndesign gate is accepted, it is provisional evidence: it does not author\nsemantic truth, earn publication authority, or satisfy promotion or closure.\nOtherwise only a disposable spike may cross the affected unresolved,\ncontradictory, or materially non-equivalent relation before design acceptance.\n\nExisting implementation placed under retrospective review may continue to\ninform the affected design. Co-evolution remains lawful only while\n`co_evolution_admissible(B)` holds; newly discovered material ambiguity,\ncontradiction, or materially non-equivalent network makes\n`decision_complete(B)` false and activates the design gate for that decision.\nPromotion and design-method closure remain blocked until the required\nOntology, three-view asset, and axiom evaluation are accepted. The design must\nevaluate the code against prior authority; it must not rewrite the Ontology or\ndiagrams merely to rationalize an unlawful implementation shape.\n\nThe asset must stay boundary-bounded. It is a defect if it mixes an active\nsemantic boundary with unrelated bootstrap, test-harness, projection, or\ndelivery shapes merely to look complete.\n\nIf the Ontology, any of the three views, the cross-view evaluation, or either\naccepted verdict is absent, the boundary is not design-method complete. Work\nmay continue as provisional co-evolution only when\n`co_evolution_admissible(B)` holds, but the affected boundary may not be\npromoted, published, or closed.\n\n### Computational Realization Projection (`STDO-UP-019`)\n\nThe three views project domain structure, execution, and lifecycle. They\ndeliberately show domain identities, so they do not reveal that two\ndomain-distinct components rest on the same data structures and algorithms.\nRepeated machinery is therefore invisible to a domain-only reading, and each\noccurrence looks correctly owned and correctly named within its own entity.\n\n**Component role sets.** Every material realization component has an explicit\nset of semantic and operational roles. Technology capability does not confer\nauthority. A component's declared role set is design authority; where a role\ncarries authority, composition and use preserve the applicable authority\nrelation under `STDO-UP-004`, which is supporting law here rather than the\nowner of the role model.\n\nA component may lawfully hold several roles. Three cases must be distinguished:\nbehavior already inside an abstract declared role is ordinary variation; a new\noperational responsibility and a new semantic or authoritative role are each\ndesign changes. Only the latter two require re-entry. Use of a component\noutside its declared role set must not silently establish identity, admission,\nlifecycle, ordering, closure, or projection truth.\n\n**The projection.** At a material semantic boundary, the design must project\nthe domain design into its computational realization: abstract data types,\nidentity and key structures, algorithms, algebraic laws, mutation and effect\nboundaries, selected implementations, and domain-to-computational mappings. The\ncross-view evaluation extends to prove the domain views and this projection\ndescribe one system.\n\n**The projection is subordinate.** It reveals repeated implementation\nstructure; it cannot decide semantic identity, ownership, or authority.\nComputational similarity produces a candidate, never an adjudication:\n\n```text\ncomputational similarity\n  -> commonization candidate\n  -> semantic and operational equivalence evaluation\n  -> reuse, parameterized common relation, or justified separation\n```\n\nTwo components may share a map or an append-only sequence while differing in\nidentity and key law, ownership, ordering, admissible mutation, consistency and\nconcurrency, retention, failure semantics, access policy, lifecycle, or\nauthority over truth.\n\nA materially non-equivalent law among those dimensions requires separation.\nOther differences justify separation only when evaluation shows that\nparameterization or adaptation would weaken ownership, authority, lifecycle,\nfailure, or operational constraints. The existence of a difference does not by\nitself discharge Prime contraction or recurrence review.\n\n**Disposition is two-part.** Each material algorithmic obligation in `M(B)`\ncarries a *semantic disposition* — existing accepted relation, extension, or\nnew candidate relation — and a *realization disposition* — consume, implement\nlocally, optimize, adapt, or deliberately duplicate under stated non-semantic\nconstraints. Isolation, substrate, performance, security, and dependency\nownership are each lawful grounds for an independent implementation.\n\nThe invariant is not that equivalent code is never rebuilt. It is that a local\nrealization must not present an existing accepted semantic or authoritative\nrelation as newly owned local truth.\n\nThis law is false if any of the following holds:\n\n- two domain-named components implement materially equivalent abstract data\n  types or algorithms without a semantic disposition;\n- a generic building block becomes a new semantic authority;\n- superficially similar operations are commonized despite different identity,\n  ordering, failure, ownership, or lifecycle law; or\n- a local realization presents an existing accepted relation as newly owned\n  local truth.\n\n"},{"contextRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context","endByte":80696,"memberDigest":"sha256:6fb49e186c15a3ebd48dec6b2728a397f1cd5199c4c0d112a0d0c70a2d6346fc","memberRef":"dependency://odd-glc/application/rc4-source@5/context/standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","path":"standards/DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md","sourceLocator":"stdo://releases/v2.5.0-rc.4/","spanDigest":"sha256:144228e54940846c666784298625a839449214b31a43a7167d6005e95e75b7f2","startByte":75556,"text":"## 6A. under this method are module-owned proof lanes.\n\nThey must derive from module ownership evidence, not from code shape alone.\n\nThat means a unit test should be traceable to:\n\n- the accepted domain, sequence, and state projections\n- the requirement families the module owns\n\nUnit tests must not be authored primarily from:\n\n- helper function boundaries\n- private method layout\n- incidental branch structure\n- temporary implementation decomposition\n- mock convenience that hides the real module contract\n\nThe required question is not:\n\n- \"what functions exist to test?\"\n\nIt is:\n\n- \"what does this module own, and what proof does that ownership require?\"\n\nThis does not prohibit helper-level tests.\n\nIt does prohibit helper-level tests from becoming the canonical proof surface\nfor a module boundary.\n\nCanonical rule:\n\n- module-owned unit tests are authoritative for module proof\n- broader integration, scenario, and sandbox lanes remain downstream
